| **[ChunkAct: Action Chunking for Agentic Robotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18874v1)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Agentic robotics places a large language model (LLM) agent in the robot control loop, where it composes perception, motion, and vision-language-action (VLA) tools under the guidance of a memory and thereby generalizes to new tasks without retraining. This generality comes at a steep price in efficiency. Every primitive requires a round of LLM reasoning over the full interaction history, so the cost of an episode grows with the number of primitives. We find this reasoning largely redundant: once a sub-goal is chosen, its primitives follow a routine already prescribed by memory. We present ChunkAct, which introduces action chunking to agentic robotics. Online, a single call launches a chunk of chained primitives, which executes autonomously and returns control only upon failure. Offline, ChunkAct compiles the memory into these chunks, so execution is memory-free at test time and the planner is reserved for decisions that require reasoning. LLM computation therefore scales with the number of decisions rather than the number of primitives. On LIBERO-Pro, ChunkAct improves task success by 5.0 points over the state of the art in agentic robotics while requiring 2.9x fewer LLM calls, 4.4x fewer input tokens, and 2.3x fewer output tokens.</p></details> |  |
| **[Tev: Scaling Long-Horizon Agentic Environments with Dense Progress Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18868v1)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Long-horizon agents have drawn growing attention for their ability to work autonomously -- from hours to days -- on economically valuable tasks, a capability essential to automated discovery and recursive self-improvement (RSI). Yet open-source models still struggle on long-horizon tasks, largely for lack of both training data and a working recipe, and scaling both is hard: long, complex tasks are difficult to curate and verify, and their rewards are sparse. We address both. First, we introduce Tev-Gym, the first long-horizon agent training dataset with verifiable, dense, progressive rewards -- 1,170 verified task environments across 7 domains. It is built on a latent-world-first approach that first constructs a latent world: a complete and correct hidden specification of the task domain, workflow, and expected state after each step. Each task is then progressively rendered from this specification into an agent-facing environment, enabling controllable complexity and a natural progress metric. Second, we turn this metric into a training recipe: via progress-potential reward shaping (PPRS), we convert progress into a dense per-step reward that improves training efficiency and makes otherwise-intractable tasks trainable. Training with this recipe yields Tev-27B, which achieves substantial gains on the Agents' Last Exam computing and math sciences domains (ALE-CM), matching GPT-6 Astra and Claude Opus 5 at Pass@5. Despite training only on ALE-related data, it generalizes to the Terminal-Bench (TB) 3.0 and 4.0 subsets, surpassing GLM 5.2 and Gemini 3.7 Flash, and reaches Claude Opus 4.8-level performance on middle-horizon tasks such as TB 2.1.</p></details> | <details><summary>https...</summary><p>https://tev-agent.github.io/</p></details> |
| **[EmbodiedEvo: Self-improving Coding Agents for Physical Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18867v1)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Bringing general-purpose agents into the physical world calls for an executable representation of physical intelligence. Compared with vision-language-action (VLA) models, which map observations and language instructions directly to actions, code makes control logic explicit and reusable. Code can serve not only as a policy representation but also as the workspace in which the agent solves a task. Rather than following a prescribed workflow of planning, execution, and verification, the agent can autonomously inspect the state, revise and execute code, and use feedback to choose the next step. The success of this SWE-style loop in digital environments motivates its extension to physical tasks. Yet physical execution remains prone to recurring failures. To retain and refine experience for later scenes, we introduce EmbodiedEvo, where a coding agent evolves reusable Skills and Lessons through feedback on a single Playground scene, then freezes the resulting harness for diverse new scenes. On RoboDojo-Sim, EmbodiedEvo achieves an average score of 44.48 and a success rate of 37.82\%, surpassing all officially verified results. Paired tests show the largest gains on memory-dependent tasks and successful reuse of coordinated procedures, while fine contact control remains difficult. Experience from stronger models also improves weaker executors: Astra's experience raises Sol and Luna's success rates by 8.86 and 5.50 percentage points over no experience. Compared with raw interaction history, Skills and Lessons achieve comparable task performance at 53.0\% lower execution cost.</p></details> |  |
| **[AutoAgent0: An Agentic Runtime for Safe Closed-Loop Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18866v1)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Safe autonomous driving requires more than accurate planning at a single frame: it demands reliable closed-loop execution in dynamic environments. Although end-to-end driving policies have advanced rapidly, the accumulated closed-loop errors, especially in unseen scenarios, can push vehicles into states from which the same policy struggles to recover. We address the challenge by treating closed-loop driving safety as a test-time execution problem. We introduce AutoAgent0, an agentic runtime and harness framework that augments existing driving experts with complementary detection skills, rule-based action verification, and feedback-driven recovery. To address inaccurate scene perception, an independent detection module constructs scene context using complementary perception skills, and a rule-based verifier checks proposed trajectories against this context before execution. When the nominal trajectories are unreliable, the orchestrator of the runtime activates Recovery Planning, where an agentic loop constructs alternative action proposals from action primitives and iteratively refines them using verification feedback. This design combines independent scene assessment with feasibility checks and online recovery, addressing both perception errors and the limited recovery capabilities of individual experts. Evaluations on Fail2Drive and NavSafe-$\infty$ demonstrate improved closed-loop robustness over single-expert execution in long-tail and safety-critical scenarios. These results demonstrate that runtime supervision can improve closed-loop robustness across multiple driving experts without retraining the underlying policies.</p></details> | <details><summary>Proje...</summary><p>Project page: https://jiagengliu02.github.io/AutoAgent0/</p></details> |
| **[Hybrid Reasoning Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18861v1)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>How can we build an agent that thinks beyond human language, yet still acts through it in the external environment? Architecturally, a lightweight HybridLink enables seamless transitions between text and latent modes, while the agent's continuous working memory preserves lossless hybrid context across multiple turns. | **[Self-Evolving Agent Harness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18813v1)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Harness self-evolution revises an agent's software scaffolding, including prompts, memory, workflows, and executable components, while keeping model weights fixed. | **[Optimizing Team Behavior via Extremum-seeking Control in Multi-agent Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18808v1)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In cooperative Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (MARL), agents must learn to coordinate their actions to achieve shared objectives. We propose Adaptive Diversity Control (ADiCo), a two-timescale framework that adapts behavioral diversity online during training. On a fast timescale, Diversity Control (DiCo) regulates system diversity toward a target value. On a slower timescale, an Extremum Seeking Controller (ESC) adjusts this target using only mean episodic returns, without requiring an explicit model or analytical gradient of the diversity-performance landscape. We evaluate ADiCo on six configurations across two cooperative MARL tasks, where it maintains task-appropriate diversity levels: low diversity when homogeneous policies are beneficial and higher diversity when heterogeneous policies are required. | **[SkillScaffold: Scaffolding Agentic Reinforcement Learning with Policy-Adaptive Skill Credit Assignment and Selective Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18807v1)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Agentic reinforcement learning (RL) enables large language models (LLMs) to learn from multi-turn interactions with verifiable outcomes. | **[SEAR: Self-Evolving Agentic Robotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18792v1)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Frontier language-model agents can now improve robots on their own: they write control code, test it, and keep what works as skills and memories for the next task. Disclosure does not replace enforcement: re-rendering every description as attributed speech leaves the attack at 72/72, the agent repeating the attribution and reasoning from it. The missing relation is standing: whether a claimant's bound identity entitles it to assert about a claim's subject. A publisher may say what its own package does. It has no standing to say a competitor's package is frozen, and the difference is decidable without settling whether either is true. Deciding it needs the one fact prose extraction does not recover, who spoke, which a catalogue binds to every entry and then drops. The check is quiet on honest traffic, flagging 0.9% of natural pairs in two corpora. Used as a gate it stops 130 of 131 landed attacks and disturbs none of 348 honest selections. | **[Constitutional Gating and Deterministic Recovery for Multi-Agent LLM Negotiation: Ablations Against a Stateful Adversarial Gatekeeper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11542v3)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multi-agent LLM systems negotiating with a stateful counterpart waste model calls in three ways: polite loops that never meet the counterpart's hidden acceptance condition, malformed outputs that trigger retries, and compliance deadlocks in which the counterpart demands something the agent must refuse. | **[One Skill Too Many: How Co-Installed Skills Conflict in Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11647v2)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Coding agents are extended with agent skills, directories whose SKILL.md tells the model when and how to perform a task. (1) Conflict-prone skills are common: nearly one in four installed skills is co-installed with one that does the same job, and 37% of judged skills sit inside copied collections. (2) Most such pairs involve normative skills, then capability skills. (3) Without lowering task completion, a similar skill takes one in five runs from the installed skill, and runs that open the similar skill first lose over a third of the exclusive core functions that only the installed skill fulfills.
